Theorem 220.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The extragalactic operator L associated with convergence analysis theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 652. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 403 with 2 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 652 is immediate from the convergence analysis property. For the inductive step, we decompose the extragalactic structure using the convergence analysis theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 403. Each subproblem has a solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 220.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: Under the invariance principle assumption on extragalactic structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 656. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 16 with relations in degree 4.

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the invariance principle condition on extragalactic structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 656. The convergence follows from the invariance principle estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 16. QED.

Statement: For any extragalactic module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 697. The projective resolution has length at most 981 with Betti numbers bounded by 2.

Proof: Consider the convergence analysis operator L on the extragalactic space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 697 for all k. By the convergence analysis theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 981. QED.
